# Qoşa açdı — накопленные результаты

## Исторический baseline из чата

До переноса исследования в GitHub было выполнено:

### 4 000 раздач × 9 простых стратегий
Предварительные наблюдения:
- удерживать все дубля до конца — слабая общая политика;
- возможность нескольких дублей за ход увеличивает темп и меняет их ценность;
- закрытая ветвь имеет стратегическую ценность;
- −10 наблюдался регулярно;
- −20 — заметно реже;
- −30/−40 в этой конкретной выборке не встретились;
- отдельно был построен пример, доказывающий возможность −40 при 3 игроках.

Эти результаты считаются историческими и не смешиваются с новыми до полной валидации нового движка.

### Разбор реальной партии seed 1898414179
Старый формат:
- schema: `qosa-round/v1`;
- engineVersion: `qosa-1.0.0`;
- players: 3;
- handSize: 9.

Для позиции было рассмотрено около 2 000 возможных скрытых раскладов. Большая часть ходов пользователя оказалась вынужденной; существенные развилки были найдены примерно на ходах №20 и №23.

### 2026-10-03 18:12 (+04)
- engine: `qosa-research-0.5.3`
- run type: first matched-seed strategy baseline
- seeds: `0..9999`; 3 seat rotations per seed; 30,000 complete rounds; 28,722 finishes; 1,278 blocks; 0 unresolved
- strategies: `random-legal`, `min-hand-pips`, `fast-doubles`; each strategy has 30,000 appearances and occupies every seat once per seed across the three rotations
- finish rate per appearance: random legal 38.17%; min-hand-pips 35.24%; fast-doubles 22.33%
- mean / median final pips: random 5.56 / 5; min-hand-pips 3.57 / 2; fast-doubles 5.11 / 4
- winner minus counts: random −10=576, −20=12; min-hand-pips −10=1,163, −20=10; fast-doubles −10=131, −20=1; no −30/−40 in this baseline
- new finding: the first simple policies expose a real objective tradeoff: immediate pip minimization substantially lowers end-of-round residue but does not maximize first-to-finish frequency; blindly prioritizing doubles is clearly weak in this three-policy environment
- confidence: medium as a reproducible baseline; the 1,000-seed pilot showed the same ordering, but this is still one bot mix with simplistic policies and fixed opening choice
- limitation: blocks are included in final-pip averages but no block winner is assigned; therefore finish rate is not an overall game-win rate
- run data: `runs/2026-10-03-baseline-0000-9999.json`
- runner: `simulator/strategy-runner.js`
- follow-up: add moderate-double-hold and branch-control policies on the same `0..9999` seed set; add block winner semantics once user confirms them


### 2026-10-03 18:16 (+04)
- engine: `qosa-research-0.5.3`
- run type: matched-seed candidate strategy extension, exact same `0..9999` seed set, 3 seat rotations per seed
- tournament A (30,000 rounds): random legal / min-hand-pips / moderate-double-hold. Finish rates: 30.50% / 30.91% / **35.16%**. Mean final pips: 7.22 / 4.32 / **3.94**. 0 unresolved.
- tournament B (30,000 rounds): random legal / min-hand-pips / closed-branch-control. Finish rates: 28.37% / 27.16% / **41.04%**. Mean final pips: 7.71 / 5.30 / **3.67**. 0 unresolved.
- strong-control tournament (30,000 rounds): min-hand-pips / moderate-double-hold / closed-branch-control. Finish rates: 26.50% / 30.23% / **39.90%**. Mean final pips: 5.61 / 4.88 / **3.87**. 0 unresolved.
- new finding: preserving closed numbers and opening as few new double-numbers as possible is the strongest provisional heuristic tested so far; importantly, it keeps its advantage when random-legal is removed and it faces the two stronger simple baselines
- secondary finding: moderate double holding improves on pure immediate pip minimization in the matched environments tested
- confidence: medium. The signal repeats across 10,000 matched seeds and stronger-opponent control, but these remain handcrafted bots rather than a proof of optimal play
- limitation: block winners remain unassigned; opening follow-up uses the common initializer policy; no rollout-based decision regret yet
- run data: `runs/2026-10-03-candidate-strategies-0000-9999.json`
- follow-up: stress-test branch control against variants that deliberately trade branch closure for tempo, then build paired decision-regret rollouts for positions with multiple legal moves


### 2026-10-03 18:50 (+04)
- engine: `qosa-research-0.5.3`
- run type: out-of-sample tempo stress test; fresh seeds `10000..19999`; 3 seat rotations; 30,000 rounds
- strategies: closed-branch-control / tempo (maximize stones shed, then pips) / tempo-open (maximize stones shed, then newly opened double numbers)
- finish rates: **46.83%** / 29.21% / 19.82%; mean final pips: **2.84** / 5.20 / 6.10; medians: **1** / 4 / 5
- blocks: 1,242 (4.14%); unresolved: 0; mean round length: 25.23 turns
- normalized minus per finish: branch-control −10 26.60%, −20 1.40%, −30 0.064%; tempo −10 14.31%, −20 0.23%; tempo-open −10 2.77%, −20 0.034%
- new finding: the closed-branch-control signal survives a fresh 10,000-seed out-of-sample set and two explicit tempo challengers. Aggressively opening numbers for tempo is especially weak in this model.
- interpretation: preserving closed numbers appears to buy both finish probability and lower remainder, while also increasing opportunities to finish on retained doubles; this remains a bot-policy hypothesis, not proof of optimal human play.
- confidence: medium-high for direction of this heuristic within the validated 3-player model; limitations are simple deterministic policies, default opening follow-up, and no assigned winner on blocks.
- run data: `runs/2026-10-03-tempo-stress-10000-19999.json`
- next: instrument starting/ending doubles and specific-double ownership, then add paired decision-regret rollouts at multi-choice states to learn when branch preservation should be overridden.


### 2026-10-03 19:47 (+04)
- engine: `qosa-research-0.5.3`
- run type: fresh-seed double-ownership instrumentation; seeds `20000..29999`; 3 seat rotations; 30,000 rounds
- strategies: min-hand-pips / moderate-double-hold / closed-branch-control; finishes 28,962; blocks 1,038; unresolved 0
- starting doubles are measured from the raw 9-tile deal before mandatory `1-1` opening/draw; mean = 2.250 for every strategy by matched-seat construction
- strongest new signal: starting with more doubles is strongly associated with finishing first in all three policies. For closed-branch-control, finish rate rises from 4.1% with 0 starting doubles to 21.1% with 1, 36.1% with 2, 53.2% with 3, 64.9% with 4 and 76.2% with 5 (6+ sample is too small for inference)
- the same monotonic pattern appears for min-hand-pips and moderate-double-hold, so this is not unique to the current branch-control heuristic
- specific-double ownership is much weaker than double count: under branch-control, conditional finish rates for owning 1-1 through 6-6 cluster around 47.0–48.7%, while 0-0 is 49.3%; these raw conditionals are confounded by total double count and starter status and are not yet interpreted as causal value
- ending doubles are rare among non-finish states: mean 0.0125 / 0.0107 / 0.0202 per appearance respectively
- confidence: high that starting-double count is a strong predictive feature in this simulator; low for causal value of any particular double until matched/controlled analysis removes hand-strength and starter confounding
- limitation: block winners remain unassigned; no decision-regret rollouts yet
- run data: `runs/2026-10-03-double-ownership-20000-29999.json`
- next: control specific-double value for total starting-double count/starter, then instrument multi-choice states for paired rollout regret

### 2026-10-23 12:52:21 +04:00 — same-tile different-branch geometry paired discovery + independent holdout
- Engine/rules: `qosa-research-0.5.3` / recovered `qosa-1.0.0`; primary 3×9. No gameplay-rule changes. Executed deterministic V8 CommonJS regression and same-tile geometry fixture; Node CLI not run.
- New runner: `simulator/same-tile-geometry-runner.js`. It holds the **exact ordinary tile, discarded pips, remaining hand, opened numbers, and hidden world constant** and varies only the legal branch end receiving that tile. Compare the placement yielding more individually playable stones in the focal remaining hand against the placement yielding fewer; require 3+ stones pre-move. This removes the previous double-versus-single tile/pip confound for the narrower question of ordinary-tile placement geometry.
- Discovery: seed window `1312940..1313589`, 120 reachable focal positions, 5760 matched hidden allocations, 11520 counterfactual continuations; profile discovery scanned {"min-hand-pips":34,"closed-branch-control":106} seed counts. min-hand-pips: n=60, higher-own-mobility minus lower-own-mobility finish-first 5.07 pp (exploratory position-cluster 95% half-width 4.81 pp), remainder -0.63 pips, next-pass -6.01 pp, position signs 24/21/15 (high/low/tie); closed-branch-control: n=60, higher-own-mobility minus lower-own-mobility finish-first 9.20 pp (exploratory position-cluster 95% half-width 6.13 pp), remainder -1.86 pips, next-pass -0.42 pp, position signs 29/17/14 (high/low/tie).
- Independent holdout: disjoint seed window `1313590..1314239`, 120 positions, 5760 matched hidden allocations, 11520 continuations; scanned {"min-hand-pips":31,"closed-branch-control":103}. min-hand-pips: n=60, higher-own-mobility minus lower-own-mobility finish-first 2.33 pp (exploratory position-cluster 95% half-width 5.03 pp), remainder -0.09 pips, next-pass -3.02 pp, position signs 29/21/10 (high/low/tie); closed-branch-control: n=60, higher-own-mobility minus lower-own-mobility finish-first 5.31 pp (exploratory position-cluster 95% half-width 6.58 pp), remainder -0.81 pips, next-pass -14.83 pp, position signs 26/15/19 (high/low/tie).
- Finding: higher immediate **own** single-stone mobility had a positive mean finish-first effect under both profiles in both seed sets, but the independent holdout intervals include zero and many individual positions favor lower own mobility. **Provisional heuristic, not a universal rule**. A human-useful testable idea: when the *same* stone can go on two different numbered ends, prefer the end that preserves more playable stones in your own hand, unless opponent access/tempo creates a counterexample.
- Confidence/limits: directional replication with 60 positions/profile/cohort, but no multiplicity correction; correlated worlds and first-eligible selection; opponent prior-choice likelihood ignored in pass-conditioned uniform worlds; simplistic fixed bot continuation; block winner unassigned; finish-first not match win; branch opening interpretation still provisional. Geometry fixture validates internal consistency, not authentic human rules.
- Cumulative: **290,100** independent full continuations unchanged; paired counterfactual continuations **343,168** (previous 320,128 + new 23,040); 11,520 new hidden allocations. Historical 4,000 separate. No new independent full strategy deals credited.
- Reproduce: `node qosha-research/simulator/same-tile-geometry-runner.js test`; `node qosha-research/simulator/same-tile-geometry-runner.js 1312940 650 60 48`; repeat with `1313590 650 60 48`. Full position-level rows and seed formula: `runs/2026-10-23-1248-same-tile-geometry-1312940-1313590.json`.

For \`c=0/1\` no two-way choice exists. Full per-stratum totals and pass probabilities are emitted by the script.
- **Validation:** exact hand counts sum to 4,686,825; two independent 48,620-partition brute-force opponent enumerations using the branch/opening legal-action kernel matched **5,005** pass-compatible worlds for owned 0-0 after 0-1, versus **2,002** for unowned 2-2 after 1-2 in the same 9-tile hand. Six-connector boundary yields 100% vs 50% immediate pass; duplicate/played-1-1 guard. A local Node check with a mirrored subset of the repository engine passed; **the committed script has not been executed from a fresh repository checkout**.
- **Finding:** retaining the matching double creates a mathematically exact **immediate pass-control effect**, independent of the unconfirmed late-arrival branch rule. It is an opening-only information/tempo feature, **not a recommendation to always keep doubles**; connector pip costs, later mobility, opponent choice, minus, block and first-place effects are not evaluated.
- **Confidence/limitations:** exact under uniformly allocated unseen opponent hands, with no assumption about which legal move an opponent chooses. The confirmed opening lock is sufficient; human opponent preferences, future branch rules, blocked ties, \`loneZero\`, bust and 101 scoring remain unresolved. No new reliable winning tactic.

| Reused seed range | Seed draws | Unique worlds | Duplicate pairs excluded | Eligible worlds | Candidate comparisons | Low/high safety flips | Canonical permutation mismatches | Numeric eligibility changes | Numeric classification mismatches / both-eligible comparisons |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 3400264–3402311 (prior 16:54 cohort) | 2,048 | 2,046 | 2 | 111 | 237 | 81/237 (34.18%) | 0/2664 | 192/2664 | 264/2472 (10.68%) |
| 3402312–3404359 (prior 17:53 cohort) | 2,048 | 2,048 | 0 | 111 | 239 | 106/239 (44.35%) | 0/2664 | 136/2664 | 188/2528 (7.44%) |
| **Combined descriptive total** | **4,096** | **4,094** | **2** | **222** | **476** | **187/476 (39.29%)** | **0/5328** | **328/5328** | **452/5000 (9.04%)** |

- Raw numeric side order changed eligibility in **328/5328** checks and changed candidate classification in **452/5000** comparisons where both runs remained eligible.

## Duplicate-world audit

The finite conditional allocation space permits different seeds to produce the same hidden assignment; seed count is not unique-world count. In the first replay range:
- seed `3401407` duplicates `3400549`;
- seed `3401867` duplicates `3400594`.

Both duplicate pairs were across development→holdout, and both worlds failed the candidate-eligibility gate. The v2 runner retains the seed audit, counts unique allocations, and excludes later duplicate allocations from analysis; headline eligible/candidate metrics therefore remain unchanged here. The regression test explicitly checks both duplicate pairs and no longer assumes every seed must be unique.

## Validation, confidence, and limits

- Exact committed source was fetched from GitHub and executed in an isolated V8 harness. Smoke test passed, including deterministic replay, 24-permutation canonical invariance, duplicate accounting, and the two duplicate-pair regressions.
- A clean local Node 22 checkout/CLI remains blocked because this runtime cannot resolve `github.com`; do not describe this as a clean Node CLI run.
- High confidence for the deterministic/metamorphic bookkeeping on these fixed synthetic worlds; low external validity for human play. Candidate rows are correlated and selected; branch reopening and blocked-tie scoring remain provisional. No first-place, final-pip, minus, block, or full-game regret result. The safety-flip percentages are conditional descriptive counts, not strategy effects.

# Matched branch-lock and block-winner sensitivity — 2026-11-01 22:54:59 +04 (Asia/Baku)

## Reproducible cohorts

- Engine/rules: `qosha-research-0.5.3`; recovered `qosa-1.0.0`, 3×9 + stock. Global number-unlock remains **PROVISIONAL**.
- Seeds `3415120–3415619`: **500 seed draws, 500 unique complete allocations, 0 duplicates**. Two policy mixes × three seat rotations = **3,000 rounds**: 2,896 finishes / 104 blocks / 0 unresolved; mean turn counter 25.239. Minus finishes: 0: 2,407; −10: 476; −20: 13; −30: 0; −40: 0.
- This is a matched branch-side/block sensitivity cohort, not a strategy tournament.

## Branch-lock sensitivity

On 6,200 nonterminal multi-side unlock events along the same global-model trajectories, the one-step **local-side-only** counterfactual reduced the aggregate next-player legal-action count from **25,965 to 15,236** (−10,729; **41.3%**). A local restriction removed at least one action in 5,171/6,200 events (**83.4%**); side choice changed in 2,878 (**46.4%**), but tile-group choice changed in only 38 (**0.6%**). There were 0 pass flips and 7 mixed-finish losses.

This is an instantaneous counterfactual on global-model trajectories—not a full local-rule rollout. Only the newly opened number is restricted to one matching side; other opened numbers remain global. It does not authenticate the physical rule.

## Block-winner identity sensitivity

Among the 104 coded blocks:
- Minimum-pip candidate: 101 unique minima, 3 tied.
- Fewest-tile-count contrast: 18 unique minima, 86 tied.
- Exact candidate seat sets agreed in only 14/104 (**13.5%**) and differed in 90/104 (**86.5%**).
- The final passer was in the minimum-pip set in 33/104 (**31.7%**) and outside it in 71.

These are deliberately labelled candidate identities, not asserted scoring rules. The current model continues to assign **no block winner**; no points or match outcome were calculated.

## Deduplicated one-turn unlock-delay diagnostic

- Discovery used 80 unique complete deals and 80 selected positions in actual windows `3415620–3415659` (min-hand-pips) and `3415920–3415959` (closed-branch-control); the wider reserved range was not fully consumed.
- 1,920 hidden-world draws yielded **1,878 unique complete allocations within visible states** and **42 duplicate draws** (2.2%); mean unique worlds/state 23.475, mean draw ESS 23.13/24. All duplicate seed pairs are recorded in the JSON.
- After per-state deduplication: **3,756 paired counterfactual continuations**. Immediate minus delayed focal finish-first: min-hand-pips **0.00 ±2.14 pp**; closed-branch-control **−0.44 ±3.27 pp**. Both position-cluster intervals include zero. Mean remainder differences were −0.045 ±0.223 and +0.028 ±0.419 pips, respectively.
- Mechanical next-opponent effect remained: immediate unlock reduced pass probability by about **6.61 pp / 15.80 pp** and increased individually playable tiles by **1.346 / 1.734** under the two continuation profiles. This does not imply a human tactic; the delayed arm is explicitly **not a legal move**.

## Regression and confidence

- Replayed the existing branch-lock reachability cohort `3406720–3407219`: 500 unique allocations, 11,795 reachable positions, 41,451/41,451 action applications, 924 passes, 476 finishes / 24 blocks, 0 invariant failures. This cohort was already recorded in RESULTS and is **not counted again**.
- Tests passed: new block-winner runner (8 assertions), deduplicated hidden-world runner (5), block fixtures (22), mixed-finish/block fixtures (11), scoring contract (1,056), plus branch-block, branch-side, unlock-delay and chronology regression tests.
- Confidence is high for internal consistency and the measured sensitivity under these policies; there is **no reliable tactical advantage** and no external authentication of branch or block rules.

## Cumulative scale

- Branch-label metamorphic cohorts remain 1,500 unique deals / 60,274 reachable positions / 16,080 action-set checks / 40,800 transitions / 4,320 pass transitions, 0 mismatches.
- Validation/opening/tie-break allocations remain 7,550; property audit 52,315 positions; branch-lock audit 550 unique deals. The 500-deal replay above is historical, not additive.

- Duplicate seed pairs are preserved in raw JSON. Uniqueness is within each visible state, not global across states.

## Matched one-turn unlock-delay mechanism

Immediate arm plays the same legal single double and unlocks its number. Delayed arm artificially keeps the number locked for the next opponent turn, then unlocks it. **The delayed arm is not a legal move**; this is mechanism sensitivity, not a tactic recommendation. Values are immediate minus delayed; approximate 95% half-widths are clustered by selected position (12 positions/cell).

| Continuation profile | Next hand | Unique worlds | Next-opponent finish Δ | Focal finish-first Δ | Next-pass Δ | Playable tiles Δ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| min-hand-pips | 1 | 30 | 31.25 ± 17.66 pp | 0.00 ± 0.00 pp | -0.31 | 0.31 |
| min-hand-pips | 2 | 70 | 0.00 ± 0.00 pp | -1.39 ± 2.72 pp | -0.38 | 0.87 |
| min-hand-pips | 3-4 | 210 | 0.00 ± 0.00 pp | -1.31 ± 4.48 pp | -0.04 | 1.11 |
| closed-branch-control | 1 | 31 | 36.11 ± 15.49 pp | -4.17 ± 8.17 pp | -0.36 | 0.36 |
| closed-branch-control | 2 | 87 | 13.33 ± 18.27 pp | -0.83 ± 1.63 pp | -0.31 | 0.78 |
| closed-branch-control | 3-4 | 201 | 0.00 ± 0.00 pp | -3.58 ± 9.49 pp | -0.18 | 1.24 |

### Finding and confidence

The strongest mechanistic signal is the **one-stone next-opponent stratum**: immediate unlock increased modeled next-opponent immediate-finish chance by 31.25 ± 17.66 pp under min-hand-pips and 36.11 ± 15.49 pp under closed-branch-control. Direction agrees across these two simple policies. However, only 12 position clusters/cell were sampled, and hidden support is small in the one-stone stratum (mean 2.50 / 2.58 unique worlds per position).

No reliable focal finish-first change was detected: all focal finish-first intervals include zero. No strategy is promoted. This is provisional to global number-unlock semantics, pass-only posterior conditioning, and synthetic opponent policies; it does not authenticate physical branch rules.

## Regression

The new runner's deterministic replay/dedup/allocation test passed (8 assertions).

- Previous unlock-delay diagnostic remains separate: 80 positions / 1,878 within-state unique allocations. Across both cohorts: 152 positions and 2,507 within-state unique allocation records (not globally deduplicated).
- Branch-label metamorphic cohorts remain 1,500 deals / 60,274 reachable positions; validation/opening/tie-break allocations 7,550; property audit 52,315 positions; strategy tournament 1,500 deals / 18,000 arm-rounds (+120-deal pilot). No strategy arms added.

# Opponent-choice prior-grid sensitivity — 2026-11-02T13:10:52+04:00 (Asia/Baku)

## Engine, rules, and seeds

- Engine: qosha-research-0.5.3 + trace-instrumented pass-history support + common-world regret evaluator + prior-grid weighting.
- Rules: recovered qosa-1.0.0, primary 3 players × 9 stones. Global-ever-open/current-only remain provisional.
- Fresh disjoint seed window: 3521601–3537600; scanned through 3533278.
- Unique deal allocations: 11678; duplicates: 0. Discovery produced 1500 selected late states and 211 prepared joint multi-action positions; the final sample contains 100 positions (50 with opponent hands 3–3, 50 with 2–3).
- Current-only-counterfactual discovery outcomes: 390 finishes / 11288 blocks / 0 unresolved. These are model-specific discovery counts, not real-game rates.

## Exact support and paired continuations

- Exact support over selected states: 1500 labeled allocations; global 570; current-only 1112; joint 570; global-only 0; current-only-exclusive 542; neither 388.
- 570 joint state-world assignments; within-state allocations are exact and deduplicated.
- Opponent-policy priors: uniform (1/3 each), random-legal-heavy (0.60 / 0.20 / 0.20), fast-doubles-heavy (0.20 / 0.20 / 0.60). Stable-style assigns one latent policy per opponent per round; per-decision re-mixes at each observed decision.
- Continuation profiles: closed-branch-control, min-hand-pips, fast-doubles. 1502 candidate-world pairs / 3004 model rollouts per profile; 9012 total rollouts; 0 unresolved. Candidate arms, branch models, and continuation seeds are paired.

## Global branch model, closed-branch-control: prior sensitivity

Entries show weighted decision-regret shift versus pass-only, in percentage points; ± is a descriptive state-cluster 95% normal interval. ESS is mean effective hidden-world count after weighting.

| Prior | Stable-style shift | Per-decision shift | Mean ESS stable / per-decision |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| uniform | 1.04 ± 1.46 pp | 0.66 ± 1.00 pp | 3.32 / 3.92 |
| random-legal-heavy | 0.89 ± 1.36 pp | 0.27 ± 0.71 pp | 3.42 / 4.51 |
| fast-doubles-heavy | 1.05 ± 1.46 pp | 0.76 ± 1.14 pp | 3.31 / 3.71 |

Across the three priors, the stable-style point estimate ranges by only 0.16 pp; per-decision by 0.49 pp. All reported intervals include zero. This does not show a reliable effect of opponent-choice weighting on candidate regret. Mean ESS is only about 3.3–4.5 worlds, so posterior concentration still depends on the assumed action model.

## Finding and limitations

The likelihood assumes random-legal is uniform over all legal actions and the other policies are uniform over top-ranked actions. The states are selected late positions generated under a current-only counterfactual; forced-pass support is exact only for the specified masks. Branch semantics, block scoring, and scoring rules remain unauthenticated. This is model sensitivity, not tactical advice. No strategy promoted.

## Validation and cumulative scale

- Regression: 9 existing likelihood assertions + 11 prior-grid assertions passed (20 total) in a V8 harness.
- Cumulative separate pass-history cohorts: 64706 deal allocations, 874 selected joint-support multi-action states, 4741 state-world assignments. These are additive cohort summaries; no global dedup across cohorts or state-world assignments.
- Clean local Node checkout remains blocked by GitHub DNS. Live scoreboard/PWA files were not touched.

## Exact next action

Use a fresh disjoint holdout to replace the uniform-top-action likelihood with a softmax/temperature family, comparing stable-style and per-decision posterior shifts on identical hidden worlds and continuation seeds. Keep priors fixed to this grid for that test; continue seeking an authenticated branch/block ruling. No strategy promotion.

- **Synthetic design:** y_gi = μ + u_g + e_gi, μ=0, u_g~N(0,1), e_gi~N(0,0.5²); decision-weighted mean; CR1 formula from the calibration module. G=4/8/20/50, cluster sizes balanced n=4, random 1–12, or half 1/half 12; 30,000 replications/configuration (360,000 datasets), NumPy PCG64 seed 202611021756.
- **Checks:** 6 formula/invariance assertions passed; the reproducible Python script completed all 12 configurations. The actual repository Node calibration test was not run because GitHub checkout DNS is unavailable.
- **Key finding:** with G=4 and severe 1/12 imbalance, mean CR1 SE was 0.682× empirical SD; nominal 95% coverage was 67.7% using normal 1.96 and 81.1% using t(G−1). At G=50 the same case reached 93.5% / 94.1%. Few independent groups plus size imbalance can make intervals badly anti-conservative.
- **Confidence/limitations:** one Gaussian random-intercept toy model; not empirical opponent calibration and not a gameplay finding. No tactic promoted.
- **Cumulative separate game cohorts:** 88,886 deal allocations, 1,074 selected multi-action positions, 5,863 state-world assignments; unchanged additive summaries, no global dedup.
- **Exact next action:** when real round-seat traces are available, report group count and size distribution; test wild-cluster bootstrap or CR2 intervals against synthetic coverage before using intervals with sparse/unequal groups. Preserve round-seat train/holdout separation; do not claim calibrated temperature without real held-out choices.

# Score-floor and round-kernel regression checkpoint — 2026-11-02T19:08:35+04:00 (Asia/Baku)

## Scope

This is a deterministic implementation-contract audit, not a gameplay cohort. No deals were simulated and no strategy was compared. The primary context remains recovered `qosa-1.0.0`, 3 players × 9 stones; four-branch opening and block/scoring interpretations remain provisional where noted in `RULES.md`.

## Tests run

Node v22.16.0. Repository source files were fetched from GitHub and their Git blob hashes were verified before running the copied modules in an isolated temporary directory; a full git clone was unavailable because the container could not resolve GitHub DNS.

- New `score-floor-contract-test.js`: **41,237 assertions passed**. Exhaustive integer score states 0–101 × deltas −40…+40 (8,262 pairs), floor/nonnegative/idempotence/exact-delta checks, monotonicity across 8,181 adjacent-score pairs, valid −10/−20/−30/−40 finish values, and invalid finish-minus inputs.
- Existing `turn-test.js`: passed (draw one then play-or-pass, stock exhaustion, pass counting, block gate).
- Existing `four-branch-lock-kernel-test.js`: passed; 24 orders for a four-double sequence, locked/unlocked number behavior and a synthetic −40 finish.
- Existing `block-score-fixtures.js`: passed, 22 assertions; per-seat pip remainders, unique/tied minimum, zero-pip hand, stock/pass threshold. The model intentionally does not infer a block winner.
- Existing `finish-minus-test.js`: passed for 1–4 ending doubles.

Verified source blob hashes: `engine.js` 82e5bd544a57683d5b4f7812beb2d4abf80da894; `turn-kernel.js` 94657896e1f8e471ceb2e9a65d1842b6102dff18; `turn-dispatcher.js` 55b0bcf3f1653c64b4e06bc95d7e917a480562a0; `block-score-fixtures.js` 539a65046f6d8dfb7ee98afad8e17463de06f2a9; `finish-minus-test.js` 4903f9ba2789773bed6f81e96d83589cfcf72bff; `turn-test.js` 598d08fa4e593bf7d3eee376108d7d574f1ec820; `four-branch-lock-kernel-test.js` c06fc39a13babd0dc70baa6ed217d92a094bac64.

## Finding

The implemented score floor and finish-minus mapping satisfy the tested finite contracts. The draw/pass, four-branch lock/open, block-remainder and −10…−40 checks are internally consistent in the research kernel. This is not confirmation of physical table rules and yields **no new tactical finding**.

The current research kernel resolves round outcomes and a score-delta floor, but does not yet implement a complete match-level scorer for the `loneZero=10` setting or 101-point bust. Those semantics must not be inferred from the parameter names. Block winner/tie allocation also remains unauthenticated.

## Scale and limitations

- New deals: 0; seed set: none; strategies: none.
- Separate historical/new game cohorts unchanged: 88,886 deal allocations, 1,074 selected multi-action positions, 5,863 state-world assignments; additive cohort summaries, no global deduplication.
- Confidence: exact finite test coverage of the coded contracts only. No physical-rule authentication, match-level scoring inference, or strategy promotion.

## Exact next action

Clarify the intended meaning and timing of `loneZero=10` and the 101-point bust rule before adding match scoring. Then add a match-score integration fixture that keeps score floor separate from block-winner assumptions; continue branch/block rule validation. Do not start large strategy batches until the scoring and branch semantics are sufficiently authenticated.

# Opening-to-round-driver integration and full-round invariant smoke — 2026-11-03T00:54:58+04:00 (Asia/Baku)

## Scope

- Engine/rules: qosha-research-0.5.3; recovered qosa-1.0.0; primary 3 players × 9 stones plus one stock tile.
- This is transition validation, not strategy evaluation or physical-rule authentication.
- Exact GitHub source blob hashes for engine.js, initializer.js, turn-kernel.js, turn-dispatcher.js, and round-driver.js were verified in the isolated Node v22.16.0 harness before execution.

## Opening-to-round-driver cycle fixtures

A new integration test exercises two constructed, tile-identity-complete 3×9 deals:

1. Mandatory follow-up: the 1-1 owner draws the sole stock tile, follows with 1-2, then the next two seats both play. The queue returns to the starter after exactly two turns; pass count remains 0.
2. Forced opening pass: the starter cannot follow on 1, so the initializer records pass count 1. The next seat plays 0-1; that resets the count to 0. The following seat has no legal move and passes; the queue returns to the starter with pass count 1 and no premature block.

The test checks legal action selection, clockwise advancement, turn/pass counters, the finish/block gate, and exact conservation of all 28 unique tile identities. **69 assertions passed.**

## Full-round deterministic invariant smoke

- Seed draws: 500, range 7,100,000–7,100,499.
- Unique full allocations: 500; duplicate allocation pairs: 0.
- All rounds use continuation initialization; if 1-1 is in stock, previous winner is set to seed modulo 3. Opening follow-up uses the initializer's canonical-up convention.
- Continuation policy: deterministic random-legal selection, uniform over the dispatcher action array, using local xorshift32 seeded by seed XOR 0x5a17c3e1. This is a smoke policy, not a benchmark strategy.
- **95,619 assertions passed** across every transition: legal-action existence, 28-tile count and identity conservation, turn increment, pass increment/reset, clockwise advance, and resolution before a 250-turn cap.

Observed under this coded model and smoke policy only:
- Finish: 475/500 (95.0%); block: 25/500 (5.0%); unresolved: 0.
- Round length: mean 23.758 turns, median 24, maximum 31.
- Opening follow-up played: 472; opening pass: 28; 1-1 in stock: 12.
- Finish-minus counts: 0 = 459; −10 = 16; −20/−30/−40 = 0.

These outcome frequencies are descriptive for this synthetic policy and provisional branch/block model; they are not tactical evidence and should not be compared to the historical baseline.

## Regression checks

The following isolated Node regressions also passed: initializer-test.js, turn-test.js, dispatcher-test.js, round-test.js, finish-minus-test.js, four-branch-lock-kernel-test.js, and block-score-fixtures.js (22 assertions).

## Finding and limitations

The initializer and round-driver connect consistently for the tested paths. The full smoke found no tile-conservation, pass-counter, queue-advance, or turn-limit failures. **No reliable strategic finding; no strategy was promoted.**

Physical branch lock/open semantics, blocked-round winner/tie allocation, and loneZero=10 remain unresolved. The local test harness used exact fetched engine-module blobs, but it was not a clean git clone; the runtime's repository checkout remains constrained by GitHub DNS.

## Separate cohort scale

Prior game cohorts remain separate and unchanged: 88,886 deal allocations, 1,074 selected multi-action positions, and 5,863 state-world assignments; no global deduplication across cohorts. This run adds a distinct 500-round invariant-smoke cohort and two non-random fixture scenarios.

## Exact next action

Add a reachable-state 24-branch-label permutation check for legal-action sets and mapped successor states (not same-seed action identity in automorphic states). Then expand the invariant smoke to a separately reported 5,000-seed cohort. Keep strategy comparisons gated on explicit branch/block assumptions or authenticated table rules.


Report/data/reproducers: [report](runs/2026-11-03-0054-opening-round-driver-cycle-audit.md), [JSON](runs/2026-11-03-0054-opening-round-driver-cycle-audit.json), [opening-cycle test](simulator/opening-round-cycle-test.js), [full-round smoke](simulator/full-round-invariant-smoke.js).

# Deal-clustered 1,000-seed branch-label tie-break ablation — 2026-11-03T03:54:55+04:00 (Asia/Baku)

## Design and reproducibility

- **Engine/rules:** `qosha-research-0.5.3`; recovered `qosa-1.0.0`, primary 3 players × 9 stones plus one stock tile. This is a model/metamorphic audit, not physical-rule authentication.
- **Seed set:** 3,404,520–3,405,519; 1,000 seed draws, 1,000 unique seat-labelled allocations, 0 duplicate allocation pairs. Previous-winner index = seed modulo 3.
- **Full rounds:** 3 seat rotations per deal; 3,000 paired rounds per mix, 6,000 pairs total; max 200 turns; all rounds resolved.
- **Mix 1:** random-legal / min-hand-pips / closed-branch-control. **Mix 2:** fast-doubles / moderate-double-hold / closed-branch-control.
- **Arms:** baseline uses the existing deterministic stable-key/raw action-list tie-break. Action-orbit keeps the same primary ranking, samples a best-ranked branch-feature orbit uniformly, then an action within that orbit uniformly. Random-legal uses the same seeded action in both arms.
- **Runner:** `simulator/branch-label-symmetric-tiebreak.js`, `run(3404520, 1000, 100, 200)`. Exact source blobs used: engine `82e5bd544a57683d5b4f7812b2d4abf80da894`; initializer `2fad29e516454580ec9a06115354e9866f3096e6`; turn-kernel `94657896e1f8e471ceb2e9a65d1842b6102dff18`; dispatcher `55b0bcf3f1653c64b4e06bc95d7e917a480562a0`; round-driver `fbaad78865adbddde1ea6651e6a501dc0bde3203`; strategy-runner `b7078942bc8f5dac6a8b07494ae8aaeadbdb1f5f`; ablation runner `26e4a7966641f3f0a8a254434ff1f0a42509268f`.
- **Execution note:** source modules were fetched from GitHub and executed in an isolated V8 harness. Temporary in-memory instrumentation collected per-deal paired aggregates for cluster uncertainty; no engine rule was changed. Raw per-deal rows were not retained, so the cluster sufficient statistics below are recorded explicitly.

## Branch-label audit (first 100 seeds, subset of the full seed set)

- 100 unique allocations; 2,274 sampled positions; 24 permutations each = **54,576 permutation checks**.
- 48,576 playable comparisons; option-state mismatches = 0; legal-action-set mismatches = 0.
- Distributional action checks: 48,576 per policy × 5 policies = **242,880**, with 0 mismatches.
- Raw deterministic choice mismatch under relabeling: random-legal 15.71%; min-hand-pips 14.00%; fast-doubles 12.71%; moderate-double-hold 14.34%; closed-branch-control 15.28%. These are tie-break/label sensitivity, not outcome or strategy effects.

## Matched full-round outcomes

Intervals are approximate 95% deal-clustered intervals (1,000 deal clusters; three seat rotations stay in the same cluster). Conditional winner/minus rates use only pairs where both arms finished.

| Mix | Baseline finish / block | Action-orbit finish / block | Round-kind change | Finish winner changed, both finished | Minus changed, both finished | Mean turn Δ (orbit − baseline) | Mean final-pip Δ per seat |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 2,900 / 100 | 2,902 / 98 | 122/3,000 = 4.07% (3.35–4.79%) | 589/2,840 = 20.74% (19.27–22.21%) | 238/2,840 = 8.38% (7.33–9.43%) | +0.011 (−0.054, +0.077) | −0.021 (−0.103, +0.061) |
| 2 | 2,896 / 104 | 2,899 / 101 | 145/3,000 = 4.83% (4.05–5.61%) | 632/2,825 = 22.37% (20.82–23.92%) | 321/2,825 = 11.36% (10.18–12.55%) | +0.009 (−0.055, +0.072) | +0.049 (−0.034, +0.132) |

Kind changes split into baseline-finish → orbit-block and baseline-block → orbit-finish: mix 1 = 60 / 62; mix 2 = 71 / 74. Mean turn and final-pip deltas have intervals spanning zero: no directional shift is established.

## Descriptive strategy and round metrics (not a strategy tournament)

| Mix / strategy | Finish rate baseline → orbit | Mean remainder pips baseline → orbit (median) | Mean ending doubles baseline → orbit |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 / random-legal | 26.87% → 28.13% | 7.91 → 7.74 (7 → 7) | 0.0060 → 0.0057 |
| 1 / min-hand-pips | 27.90% → 28.43% | 5.26 → 5.28 (4 → 4) | 0.0070 → 0.0070 |
| 1 / closed-branch-control | 41.90% → 40.17% | 3.54 → 3.62 (2 → 2) | 0.0127 → 0.0153 |
| 2 / fast-doubles | 18.33% → 18.33% | 6.76 → 6.74 (5 → 5) | 0.0030 → 0.0027 |
| 2 / moderate-double-hold | 33.00% → 34.30% | 4.42 → 4.38 (3 → 3) | 0.0097 → 0.0093 |
| 2 / closed-branch-control | 45.20% → 44.00% | 3.37 → 3.58 (2 → 2) | 0.0173 → 0.0177 |

Mean starting doubles were 2.00 per player in every arm. Mean round length / block rate: mix 1 baseline 25.359 turns / 3.33%, orbit 25.370 / 3.27%; mix 2 baseline 25.223 / 3.47%, orbit 25.232 / 3.37%.

Finish-minus counts (baseline → orbit):
- Mix 1: 0 = 2,466 → 2,484; −10 = 417 → 401; −20 = 17 → 17; −30 = 0 → 0; −40 = 0 → 0.
- Mix 2: 0 = 2,328 → 2,327; −10 = 540 → 544; −20 = 27 → 27; −30 = 1 → 1; −40 = 0 → 0.

## Cluster uncertainty sufficient statistics

For a rate, let deal-level numerator be `y_i`, denominator be `n_i`, estimate `p = sum(y_i) / sum(n_i)`, and `N = 1,000`. The reported SE is `sqrt((N/(N-1)) * sum((y_i - p*n_i)^2) / sum(n_i)^2)`. For deal-level means, `SE = sqrt(sum((x_i - xbar)^2) / (N*(N-1)))`. The listed sums of squares allow the displayed SEs to be reconstructed from the counts above.

| Mix | Metric | Numerator / denominator | Cluster residual/deviation sum of squares |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| 1 | Round-kind change | 122 / 3,000 | 121.116 |
| 1 | Finish-winner change | 589 / 2,840 | 455.323 |
| 1 | Minus change | 238 / 2,840 | 233.058 |
| 1 | Mean turn delta | — | 1,116.538 |
| 1 | Mean final-pip delta per seat | — | 1,746.306 |
| 2 | Round-kind change | 145 / 3,000 | 141.975 |
| 2 | Finish-winner change | 632 / 2,825 | 497.749 |
| 2 | Minus change | 321 / 2,825 | 292.100 |
| 2 | Mean turn delta | — | 1,048.369 |
| 2 | Mean final-pip delta per seat | — | 1,775.958 |

## Finding, confidence, and limitations

**Useful finding:** branch-label action sets and their tested distributions remain equivariant, but the tie-break can change a full-round outcome. Across the larger cohort, round kind changed in 4.07–4.83% of matched pairs and finish winner changed in 20.74–22.37% of pairs where both arms finished. This is representation/tie-break sensitivity only; it does not show that action-orbit is a stronger strategy. No strategy was promoted.

Confidence is high for these coded-model metamorphic and matched-seed measurements, with deal-clustered uncertainty shown. It is not physical-rule authentication. Branch lock/open semantics, block winner/tie allocation, and `loneZero=10` remain unresolved. No 5-player cohort or hidden-world Monte Carlo was added.

## Separate cohort scale and exact next action

- This run adds 1,000 unique matched-seed deals and 6,000 paired round comparisons. The first 100 audit deals are a subset, not an extra cohort.
- The previous matched ablation remains a separate 120-deal / 720-pair cohort. Together these runs represent 1,120 seed draws / 6,720 paired comparisons; no global deduplication across all research cohorts and no pooling with historical baseline samples.
- Other prior separate cohorts unchanged: 88,886 deal allocations, 1,074 selected multi-action positions, 5,863 state-world assignments, and the 5,000-round invariant smoke.
- **Exact next action:** add committed per-deal cluster-summary output plus a deterministic regression test to the ablation runner, then return to labelled block-winner/tie and branch lock/open hypotheses. Keep strategy promotion gated on physical-rule evidence or explicitly isolated model assumptions.

- **Engine/rules:** qosha-research-0.5.3; recovered qosa-1.0.0 coded model; primary 3×9 plus one stock tile. Physical branch/opening semantics remain provisional.
- **Seed retry convention:** consume monotonically increasing nonnegative integer seeds. If the first-round initializer returns the explicit 1-1-in-stock redeal signal, advance to the next seed; never retry the same deterministic seed. After an accepted deal, the next requested deal begins at the following integer. The coded PRNG maps the input seed to 32 bits; these ranges are far below wrap.

All 6,666 inventory snapshots conserved the exact 28 tile identities. There were 4,444 round-driver/manual transition equivalence checks and 4,406 played-tile provenance/uniqueness checks. No in-round draws occurred (the single stock tile is consumed during opening setup); 97 passes and 4,347 actions were observed. No premature finish or block occurred.

- **Finding/confidence:** high confidence in the tested coded initializer/round-driver contract and inventory conservation. This is not authentication of physical table rules and not a strategy comparison; no tactical finding or strategy promotion.

- **Cumulative scale:** this run adds 2,000 accepted first-round samples (2,078 attempt seeds) and a separate 2,000-seed continuation cohort. The prior 12,000,000–12,001,999 initializer audit remains a separate cohort; all other strategy/block/tie-break samples remain separate.

- **Seeds/deals:** exact replay of 20,000,000–20,001,034; 1,035 attempts, 35 redeals, 1,000 unique accepted outcomes (942 finish, 58 block); **0 new independent deals**. Deterministic legal-action hash policy, not a strategy arm.
- **Six matched orders:** 76 common match boundaries per order. Previously observed 26 rank changes in gap 11–20 = **3 unique boundaries**, indices 560/801/856 (9/9/8 dependent contrasts). All 26 involved changed pending survivor events and survivor scores; **0 sorting-only**, **0 different pre-round scores**, **0 changed exploded seat**.
- **Mechanism:** PWA ends immediately on the first 101-crossing event. Nonzero survivor deltas scheduled later in the manual entry order remain pending; this can reverse survivor ranking even if the busting seat is unchanged. A pending delta can be positive or negative (finish-minus).
- **Validation:** 28,265 trace/adapter checks; 146 regression assertions; exact full replay and 40-round smoke. All 26 traces retained in JSON. Physical rules not authenticated, no strategy promoted, historical baseline not pooled.
- **Cumulative scale:** order-sensitivity cohorts remain 4,000 accepted outcomes across four separate cohorts (3,000 in six-order holdouts); current run reuses 1,000 of them, no addition.
- **Exact next action:** read-only live PWA pending-event verification with positive and negative deltas, then prioritize physical branch/block semantics and matched-seed strategy work.

### 2026-11-06 20:49 (+04, Asia/Baku) — exact starter-confounding reference for double-count strategy analysis
- Engine/rules: `qosa-research-0.5.3` / recovered `qosa-1.0.0`; 3 players × 9 stones; no rule mutation. This is an **exact combinatorial reference**, not a simulator run.
- New simulated deals: **0**; seed set: none; strategies: none. New finish/win, remainder, length, block, minus, branch and regret metrics: **not measured**. The previously persisted 180,000 matched-seat strategy rounds remain a separate historical cohort.
- Scope: condition on `1-1` being in one of the 27 dealt stones (probability 27/28 for an unconditioned uniformly shuffled 3×9+1 deal). Its owner is the starter. Before the mandatory opening/draw, starter has `D_s = 1 + Hypergeom(N=27,K=6,n=8)` doubles; each nonstarter has `D_o = Hypergeom(N=27,K=6,n=9)`. These are marginal distributions; seats are **not independent**.
- Exact reference probabilities: `P(D_s >= 3) = 0.5940812810` versus `P(D_o >= 3) = 0.3046316003`; `P(D_s >= 4) = 0.2274450187` versus `P(D_o >= 4) = 0.0730718557`. Expected doubles: starter `1 + 8*6/27 = 2.7777777778`, nonstarter `9*6/27 = 2.0000000000`. Thus the starter has **~3.11×** the marginal chance of at least four doubles, without any strategy effect.
- New finding: the raw double-count-to-finish association in the old `20000..29999` cohort is substantially exposed to a **mechanical opening/starter confound**. These probabilities are exact hand-deal baselines only; they do not estimate a causal advantage, actual finish rate, or value of holding a specific double. For later rounds with stock `1-1` (probability 1/28), starter is the previous winner and does not own `1-1` in the raw hand; exclude that case from this formula and analyze it separately using `base.starter`.
- Confidence: exact under uniform random deal conditional on dealt `1-1`; no physical branch/block assumptions enter. No gameplay-strategy claim; no live PWA changes.
- Execution gate: local `git ls-remote https://github.com/Mikayilzade/domino-schetki.git HEAD` failed with `Could not resolve host: github.com`; **0/17 suites executed**. Source inspected: `validation-gate.js`, `validation-fingerprint-replay-test.js`, `specific-double-control.js`.
- Exact next action: obtain executable checkout, run replay and all 17 suites, archive real JSON; only after a green gate execute matched 3×9 `30000..39999` controlled-double comparison stratified by **actual** starter and total doubles, with stock-opening cases kept separate.

Let X be the number of other doubles among the starter's initial eight tiles. Then `P(X=x)=C(6,x)C(15,8-x)/C(21,8)`. Given X=x, the stock tile is a double with probability `(6-x)/13`; let S be that indicator. The specified opponent's nine tiles contain Y other doubles with `Y|X=x,S=s ~ Hypergeom(N=18,K=6-x-s,n=9)`. This is a *joint-deal combinatorial constraint*, not a tested playing tactic or win-rate effect.
